**Yes—several useful references were left out.** I compared the supplied conversation, retrieved earlier planning messages, and all ten current planning/reference documents on `servo/r0-baseline` at commit `e4f0e27`. The older conversation is available through retrieval, so this is not a guaranteed line-by-line audit of every original message.

The following references are absent from those documents:

| Missing reference | Why it belongs in our source inventory |
|---|---|
| **[SimpleFOC](https://github.com/simplefoc/Arduino-FOC)** | Previously proposed but not evaluated. Useful for FOC [field-oriented control], sensor/driver separation, current-sense alignment, motion control, and monitoring. Compatibility with our GD32F130C8 remains unverified. |
| **[mianos/esp32foc](https://github.com/mianos/esp32foc)** | **The clearest provenance omission.** Its resistance, inductance, and inverter-drop identification directly informed our commissioning discussion. Those ideas reached the roadmap, but the repository attribution did not. Its actual controller is open-loop; it should remain a commissioning reference. |
| **[VESC [Vedder Electronic Speed Controller] firmware](https://github.com/vedderb/bldc)** | Previously discussed, including its `datatypes.h`. Useful for comparing motor configuration, protection, fault reporting, and command conventions. |
| **[ODrive](https://github.com/odriverobotics/ODrive)** | Previously discussed for servo architecture and protocol compatibility. Useful for reviewing calibration and axis-control interfaces. **The public repository contains legacy v3.x firmware**, not current-generation firmware source. |
| **[OpenFFBoard](https://github.com/Ultrawipf/OpenFFBoard)** | Previously proposed for force-feedback work. Useful for motor-driver and encoder interfaces, runtime configuration, and the boundary between effect generation and the servo drive. |
| **[Bipropellant firmware](https://github.com/bipropellant/bipropellant-hoverboard-firmware)** and **[its protocol repository](https://github.com/bipropellant/bipropellant-protocol)** | Previously discussed explicitly. Useful for separating communications from motor control, machine-readable commands, interactive diagnostics, and wheel speed/position control. Both repositories deserve entries. |
| **[CommunityGD32Cores/platform-gd32](https://github.com/CommunityGD32Cores/platform-gd32)** | Missing from the source register even though our `platformio.ini` **already uses it as the build platform**. This should be documented as an actual build dependency. |
| **[CommunityGD32Cores/GD32Firmware](https://github.com/CommunityGD32Cores/GD32Firmware)** | Earlier work identified commit `4005bb036172cf671976df6160b828a3ee6e738e` for the vendor-library reference. That provenance is missing from the planning source register. |
| **[maxgerhardt/pio-gd32f130c6](https://github.com/maxgerhardt/pio-gd32f130c6)** | Explicitly cited during the PlatformIO discussion. Useful as a historical GD32F130 build example; the repository is archived. |
| **[FFBeast documentation repository](https://github.com/ffbeast/ffbeast.github.io)** | FFBeast was proposed earlier, but never entered the inventory. Record it as a force-feedback hardware/setup and behavior reference; the documentation and firmware downloads do not establish availability of reusable firmware source. |

The existing [source register](https://github.com/Arcturis144/Hoverboard-Firmware-Hack-Gen2.x-GD32-ServoFOC/blob/servo/r0-baseline/docs/provenance/SOURCES.md) **already includes** miniFOC, both EFeru motor-control repositories, and the major fork-survey findings. Those do not need duplicate entries.

I also found a related documentation error: the docs describe hoverboardhavoc’s modified library package as a reference awaiting promotion, but [our current build configuration](https://github.com/Arcturis144/Hoverboard-Firmware-Hack-Gen2.x-GD32-ServoFOC/blob/servo/r0-baseline/HoverBoardGigaDevice/platformio.ini) already selects it. Its dependency status needs correcting alongside the missing PlatformIO attribution.
